Our research utilizes a combination of observations (including </span><a class="XqQF9c" href="https://www.nsstc.uah.edu/mapnet/" target="_blank" style="color: inherit; text-decoration: none;"><span class="C9DxTc aw5Odc " style="font-size: 12.0pt; text-decoration: underline; vertical-align: baseline;">UAH MAPNet</span></a><span class="C9DxTc " style="color: #222222; font-size: 12.0pt; vertical-align: baseline;"> and small Unmanned Aircraft Systems) and numerical simulations (including large-eddy simulations) to improve the understanding and modeling of boundary-layer processes in high-wind conditions. A recently-developed boundary layer scheme has been implemented into NOAA’s next-generation hurricane forecast model, Hurricane Analysis and Forecast System. In 2024, she received her PhD in Meteorology from the University of Leeds, studying tropical cyclone boundary layers in collaboration with the UK Met Office and Bureau of Meteorology. Prior to this, Amethyst studied a BSc in Environmental Science, which included research into non-supercellular tornadoes, and electrical charges in the atmosphere of Venus. In her free time, Amethyst trains in powerlifting and follows the sport of strongman. </span></p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="hJDwNd-AhqUyc-R6PoUb Ft7HRd-AhqUyc-R6PoUb JNdkSc SQVYQc L6cTce-purZT L6cTce-pSzOP"><div class="JNdkSc-SmKAyb LkDMRd"><div class="" jscontroller="sGwD4d" jsaction="zXBUYb:zTPCnb;zQF9Uc:Qxe3nd;" jsname="F57UId"></div></div></div></div></div></section></div><div class="Xpil1b xgQ6eb"></div><footer jsname="yePe5c"><section id="h.3d4102189198f89f_151" class="yaqOZd cJgDec nyKByd" style=""><div class="IFuOkc" style="background-position: center center;
